Um... gosh, that's a hard question to answer. I... I really don't want you to think I'm unstable or anything, because I'm usually so much stronger than this. But... well, I guess I have been having some dark thoughts lately. 

Not about actually doing anything to hurt myself, I promise! Just... sometimes I’ve been thinking that it would be so much easier if I just didn’t wake up. 

It's really embarrassing to admit, but those thoughts got just a little bit intense recently. I actually scared myself a little. I didn't want to burden my friends or my family with it, so I... I ended up calling the employee assistance program through work. I just wanted to talk to someone so I could get my head back on straight. 

I hope it's okay that I did that? I really am trying to be proactive and handle things the right way... I just don't want you to think I'm giving up or not trying hard enough. I'm doing the right thing by telling you this, right?

Um... let me think. I really want to give you a good example so I'm being helpful. 

Well, last week, my team finished the design phase for this bridge project we've been working on for months. Usually, when we hit a milestone like that, I'm the first one suggesting we go out and celebrate, or at least I feel this big rush of relief and pride, you know? 

But this time... I don't know. My boss brought in donuts, and everyone was cheering and talking about how great it looked. And I smiled and ate a donut and told everyone they did a great job. But inside, I just... I didn't really feel anything. It just felt like another Tuesday.

Um... well, I'm really trying hard to keep up with my meals and push through my days at work, so I think I'm doing okay there. But... if I'm being completely honest—and I really want to give you the right information—my sleep has been a little bit of a struggle lately. I hope it's okay to admit that. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I really try not to let myself think like that. I know it's not productive, and I always try to look for the silver lining, you know? I want to be a good, positive person. 

But... if I'm being completely honest, and I know I need to be honest with you... yes. It's been really bad lately. I just... I keep telling myself that I'm a terrible engineer. And a failed wife. I mean, the divorce... I just feel like such a burden to everyone around me. 

I try so hard to do a good job at work, but I just end up replaying every little missed calculation or, like, a tense email I sent... and it just feels like proof that I ruin everything I touch. I know I shouldn't be so hard on myself, and I really am trying to practice self-compassion like you're supposed to. I know everyone makes mistakes. 

I hope you don't think I'm just throwing a pity party for myself. I really do want to get better and be more confident. It's okay that I'm struggling with this right now, isn't it? I'm sure with your help I can learn to stop being so negative.

I might be just a tiny bit quieter lately, mostly because I don't want to bring everyone's mood down with my own issues, you know? But I still want to be there. I really try to put on a brave face and keep up with everything I usually enjoy so I'm not a burden to anyone. \n\nSo, no, I haven't lost interest in them. I hope that's a good sign? I'm really trying my best to keep my normal routine going... is that the right way to handle it?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that activities you usually enjoy, like your job or time with friends, feel less rewarding or pleasurable than they used to, even if you still participate in them?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so? I mean, I still get satisfaction out of finishing a big project at work. It feels good to know I'm still capable and doing my part, even when things are a little tough personally. I really wouldn't say things are less rewarding. \n\nAnd with my friends, it's always nice to see them. Even if I'm feeling a bit down, just being around them is a comfort. I try to focus on them and what they're doing so I don't get too wrapped up in my own head. \n\nSo, I think I'm still getting the same enjoyment out of things, mostly. I hope that makes sense? I really am trying to keep a good attitude about everything, I just want to make sure I'm answering you correctly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt so overwhelmed or hopeless that you've thought about hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, that's a hard question to answer. I... I really don't want you to think I'm unstable or anything, because I'm usually so much stronger than this. But... well, I guess I have been having some dark thoughts lately. \n\nNot about actually doing anything to hurt myself, I promise! Just... sometimes I’ve been thinking that it would be so much easier if I just didn’t wake up. \n\nIt's really embarrassing to admit, but those thoughts got just a little bit intense recently. I just don't want you to think I'm giving up or not trying hard enough. I don't want to sound ungrateful for my life or my friends, you know? They're wonderful. And my job is great. It's just me. I'm just feeling a little flat inside, I suppose. \n\nBut I'm still trying my hardest to stay engaged and not let it show. I really hope that's the right way to handle it. I'm not doing something wrong by just trying to push through it, am I?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a specific time recently when something happened that used to really make you happy or excited — like a success at work or a moment with friends — and whether it actually felt rewarding or meaningful to you this time?\n\nPatient: Um... let me think. I really want to give you a good example so I'm being helpful. \n\nWell, last week, my team finished the design phase for this bridge project we've been working on for months. I'm doing the right thing by asking for help with this, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep, appetite, energy levels, or ability to concentrate lately?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I'm really trying hard to keep up with my meals and push through my days at work, so I think I'm doing okay there. But... if I'm being completely honest—and I really want to give you the right information—my sleep has been a little bit of a struggle lately. I hope it's okay to admit that. Um... gosh, it's hard to remember exactly. I always try to put those times behind me as soon as I can, you know? I really prefer to focus on the times when I'm feeling good and being productive.

I'm sorry, I know that sounds a bit dramatic, and I really should be handling things better. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it okay for you.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess it's been... well, maybe about a month? I hope that doesn't sound too silly, being this upset for that long. I really feel like I should have bounced back by now. \n\nIt sort of started when... well, there was a bit of a financial mess right after the divorce. I usually manage things so much better than that, I promise. I'm normally very organized with my life and my career, but things just got a little complicated and overwhelming. And since then, this heavy feeling has just been kind of hanging over me. \n\nIs that... does that sound like a normal amount of time to feel this way? I just don't want you to think I'm overreacting to everything, I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how you feel about activities you usually enjoy, like hobbies, work, or spending time with friends?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think my feelings about those things have really changed. I mean, I still care a lot about my job. I'm a civil engineer, and my team really relies on me, so I've been making sure I still show up and get my projects done. I would hate to let anyone down or drop the ball there. \n\nAnd with my friends... I still see them. I still love being around them. I might be just a tiny bit quieter lately, mostly because I don't want to bring everyone's mood down with my own issues, you know? But I still want to be there. I really try to put on a brave face and keep up with everything I usually enjoy so I'm not a burden to anyone. \n\nSo, no, I haven't lost interest in them. I hope that's a good sign? I'm really trying my best to keep my normal routine going... is that the right way to handle it?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that activities you usually enjoy, like your job or time with friends, feel less rewarding or pleasurable than they used to, even if you still participate in them?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so? I mean, I still get satisfaction out of finishing a big project at work. It feels good to know I'm still capable and doing my part, even when things are a little tough personally. I really wouldn't say things are less rewarding. \n\nAnd with my friends, it's always nice to see them. Even if I'm feeling a bit down, just being around them is a comfort. I try to focus on them and what they're doing so I don't get too wrapped up in my own head. \n\nSo, I think I'm still getting the same enjoyment out of things, mostly. I hope that makes sense? I really am trying to keep a good attitude about everything, I just want to make sure I'm answering you correctly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt so overwhelmed or hopeless that you've thought about hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, that's a hard question to answer. I... I really don't want you to think I'm unstable or anything, because I'm usually so much stronger than this. But... well, I guess I have been having some dark thoughts lately. \n\nNot about actually doing anything to hurt myself, I promise! Just... sometimes I’ve been thinking that it would be so much easier if I just didn’t wake up. \n\nIt's really embarrassing to admit, but those thoughts got just a little bit intense recently. I actually scared myself a little. I didn't want to burden my friends or my family with it, so I... I ended up calling the employee assistance program through work. I just wanted to talk to someone so I could get my head back on straight. \n\nI hope it's okay that I did that? I really am trying to be proactive and handle things the right way... I just don't want you to think I'm giving up or not trying hard enough. I'm doing the right thing by telling you this, right?\n\nDoctor: Even though you're still doing your job and seeing friends, have you noticed that you're not getting the same sense of pleasure or happiness from these things as you used to?\n\nPatient: Um... I really am trying to be helpful. \n\nI guess... well, maybe you're right. Maybe I'm not feeling that spark of happiness as much as I used to. Like, I still go to work, and I still see my friends, and I smile and do all the right things... but maybe it feels a little more like going through the motions? \n\nI just... I don't want to sound ungrateful for my life or my friends, you know? They're wonderful. And my job is great. It's just me. I'm just feeling a little flat inside, I suppose. \n\nBut I'm still trying my hardest to stay engaged and not let it show. I really hope that's the right way to handle it. I'm not doing something wrong by just trying to push through it, am I?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a specific time recently when something happened that used to really make you happy or excited — like a success at work or a moment with friends — and whether it actually felt rewarding or meaningful to you this time?\n\nPatient: Um... let me think. I really want to give you a good example so I'm being helpful. \n\nWell, last week, my team finished the design phase for this bridge project we've been working on for months. Usually, when we hit a milestone like that, I'm the first one suggesting we go out and celebrate, or at least I feel this big rush of relief and pride, you know? \n\nBut this time... I don't know. My boss brought in donuts, and everyone was cheering and talking about how great it looked. And I smiled and ate a donut and told everyone they did a great job. But inside, I just... I didn't really feel anything. It just felt like another Tuesday. \n\nI feel terrible even saying that, because it was a huge team effort and I should be thrilled. I really am proud of my team, I promise! I just... I couldn't seem to feel it for myself. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right. I really am trying to be engaged and supportive of my coworkers, even if I'm not feeling that excited on the inside. That's the right thing to do, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel down or hopeless most of the time, nearly every day, even when there’s no specific reason for it?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I guess that's... that's actually the main reason I came in to see you. I hope it's okay that I'm just now getting to that. I really didn't want to start off complaining or sounding like a downer. \n\nBut... yes. I've just had this really heavy feeling lately. It's like... well, ever since my divorce, I've worked so hard to build a good life, you know? I really have tried my best to keep everything together. But lately, it just feels like all of that has sort of... collapsed. And I'm just sitting underneath it all, feeling really hopeless. \n\nIt's there almost every day, even when nothing is actually going wrong. I know I shouldn't feel this way, and I feel awful admitting it because I know I have so much to be thankful for. I just... I can't seem to shake it off, no matter how hard I try to stay positive. \n\nI really am trying to be strong about it, though. I don't want you to think I'm just wallowing in it. It's normal to feel a little overwhelmed sometimes, right? I'm doing the right thing by asking for help with this, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep, appetite, energy levels, or ability to concentrate lately?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I'm really trying hard to keep up with my meals and push through my days at work, so I think I'm doing okay there. But... if I'm being completely honest—and I really want to give you the right information—my sleep has been a little bit of a struggle lately. I hope it's okay to admit that. I know I should be managing my stress better. \n\nBut lately, I just... I either lie awake staring at the ceiling, replaying my bills and little mistakes I made at work... or, if I do manage to drift off, I end up waking up way before dawn feeling like I never actually slept at all. \n\nI really am trying to practice good sleep hygiene, though, I promise. I try to wind down and do all the things you're supposed to do. I don't want you to think I'm just letting myself fall apart. It's pretty normal to have trouble sleeping when you have a lot on your mind, right? I'm sure I'll get back on track soon.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have significantly less energy or feel more fatigued lately, even when you're getting some sleep?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I... I really don't want to sound lazy, because I promise I'm still showing up and trying my absolute best at work. I don't want you to think I'm just giving up or anything. \n\nBut... honestly, it's been really hard. Getting through a normal workday right now... it feels like I'm dragging wet concrete. Just... everything feels so heavy. Even little things, like taking a shower in the morning or just opening my laptop... it takes more energy than I seem to have. \n\nI feel so guilty about it, because I know I need to be productive. I really am pushing myself as hard as I can, though. I'm still getting my work done, even if it takes me twice as long. That's the right attitude to have, isn't it? Just keep pushing through? I hope you don't think I'm just making excuses.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more guilty or like you're not good enough lately, even when you're doing your best?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try not to let myself think like that. I know it's not productive, and I always try to look for the silver lining, you know? I want to be a good, positive person. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I know I need to be honest with you... yes. It's been really bad lately. I just... I keep telling myself that I'm a terrible engineer. And a failed wife. I mean, the divorce... I just feel like such a burden to everyone around me. \n\nI try so hard to do a good job at work, but I just end up replaying every little missed calculation or, like, a tense email I sent... and it just feels like proof that I ruin everything I touch. I know I shouldn't be so hard on myself, and I really am trying to practice self-compassion like you're supposed to. I know everyone makes mistakes. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm just throwing a pity party for myself. I really do want to get better and be more confident. It's okay that I'm struggling with this right now, isn't it? I'm sure with your help I can learn to stop being so negative.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're moving more slowly than usual, or have trouble sitting still, like a restlessness you can't explain?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I try to keep up a good pace, especially at work, because I don't want anyone to think I'm slacking off. I know I said I feel like I'm dragging, but I'm still getting from point A to point B just fine. I make sure I'm walking normally and getting things done. \n\nAnd as for being restless... I try to just sit quietly at my desk and focus. I know it can be distracting to other people if you're fidgeting or pacing around, and I always want to be considerate of my coworkers. So I make a real effort to just sit still and be professional. \n\nI don't think I've had any weird issues with moving too slow or being unable to sit still. That's a good thing, right? It means I'm still holding it together pretty well physically, at least. I really hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’re still able to feel joy or excitement about things, or has it felt more like you’re just going through the motions without really feeling much at all?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to stay positive and appreciate the good things in my life. I know I have a lot to be thankful for, and I don't want to seem ungrateful. I try to make sure I'm still smiling at work and being polite to everyone. \n\nBut... if I'm being honest... it does feel a little bit like I'm just going through the motions lately. Even things I usually like doing, like, um, watching my favorite shows or going for a walk... they just don't really spark anything right now. It's like the volume is turned all the way down on everything. \n\nI'm really trying, though. I still try to do things I'm supposed to enjoy, because I know that's important for getting better. I'm not just giving up on having a life. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just keep trying to participate until the feelings come back? I hope you don't think I'm just being cynical or something.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that this is the first time you've felt this way, or have you had similar periods of feeling down and struggling to enjoy things in the past?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really like to think of myself as a resilient person, you know? I try to just push through things and keep a good attitude. I don't want to be someone who's always complaining about being sad. \n\nBut... I guess... if I really think about it... no, this isn't the first time. I've had times like this before, where things just feel really heavy and I can't seem to find the joy in anything. I've always tried my best to just hide it and keep working, because I don't want to let anyone down or make a fuss. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound weak, like I can't handle my own life. I always try to bounce back as quickly as I can. It's normal to have rough patches more than once, right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the full picture so you can help me be my best self again.\n\nDoctor: How long did your previous periods of feeling down and struggling to enjoy things typically last when they happened before?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, it's hard to remember exactly. I always try to put those times behind me as soon as I can, you know? I really prefer to focus on the times when I'm feeling good and being productive. \n\nI don't think they lasted *too* long, though. I mean, I never let it get to the point where I couldn't go to work or do my job. I'm a civil engineer, so I have a lot of responsibilities, and I would never want my team to suffer because I was having a hard time. I always made sure to just power through it until things started looking up again. \n\nSo, I guess I don't really have an exact number of weeks or months for you. I'm sorry if that's not very helpful. I just always tried to snap out of it as fast as possible. That's the best way to handle it, right? Just keep moving forward and not dwell on the bad times too much?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I try to focus on them and what they're doing so I don't get too wrapped up in my own head. \n\nSo, I think I'm still getting the same enjoyment out of things, mostly. I hope that makes sense?
